## assistant · 2026-07-12T11:42:31Z

这个思路可行，而且比我之前想的更聪明——用"人工路由"替代"自动编排"，正好绕开了 v0.2 Coordinator 和 v0.3 Room 里最复杂的两块（自动选团队、自动组织协作），只留下"多个不同 agent 可用"这一件事，而这件事技术上已经基本具备了。

## 为什么这个可行，而且不需要等 v0.2/v0.3

查了一下 F002 现在的设计：`POST /api/issues/:issue_id/runs` 这个创建 Run 的接口，本来就要求传一个 `adapter_id`——也就是说"每一次下发指令都要指定用哪个 agent 执行"这件事，后端契约已经支持了，不是要新加的能力。缺的只有两块：

1. **多接入几个 adapter**（Claude Code、OpenCode）——这个决策 0002 里写的是"P0 先只接 Codex，为了聚焦和 dogfooding",不是因为技术上做不到,只是主动选择先聚焦一个。要加并不需要 Coordinator 或 Room 这些复杂的东西撑着。
2. **composer 里加一个"@ 选择由哪个 agent/角色处理"的轻量 UI**——类似 clowder-ai 的 @mention 路由，但由你手动选，不是系统自动路由。这只是前端加一个选择器，后端的 `adapter_id` 字段本来就在等这个输入。

真正复杂、我建议继续放在 v0.2/v0.3 的东西是：Coordinator **自动**判断该找谁、Workflow/Topology **自动**推荐、Room 里agent之间**自动**讨论分工——这些你的方案里都不需要，因为"该找谁"这个判断由你自己做。

而且从协作拓扑的角度看，你这个方案其实还是 PRD 定义的 `sequential`（顺序式）拓扑，只是每一步"该交给谁"从"Workflow Template 固定写死的顺序"变成"你手动 @ 指定"——概念上完全没有超出 v0.1 的范围，只是需要多个 adapter 可选。

## 我的建议

把这个做成 v0.1 序列里的一个新子版本，比如 **v0.1.4：手动多 Agent 路由**，范围大致是：

- 接入 Claude Code、OpenCode 至少一个（凑够"真的不同"的角色，不然自己跟自己协同没意义）。
- Thread composer 里加一个角色/agent 选择器（@ 或下拉都行），每次下发指令时明确选定由谁处理。
- 上一个角色的结论（Handoff Packet + evidence refs，F003 已经有）自动成为下一个被 @ 的角色的上下文，你不用手动复制——这是这个方案真正解决"传话筒"问题的地方。
- 不做：Coordinator 自动推荐、Workflow/Topology 自动选择、Room 可视化协作现场——这些留给 v0.2/v0.3。

这样调整的话，PRD 第 15 节需要把"多 agent adapter 补齐"这一条从 v0.2 的范围里挪一部分到新的 v0.1.4（v0.2 保留的是"自动推荐"这部分，adapter 本身已经在 v0.1.4 就有了）。

这个方向要不要我现在就改进 PRD？改完之后 F005（v0.1.4 对应的 feature）的具体 spec 还是等 F001-F004 做完、按我们"按需拆解"的节奏再写，现在只调整路线图。

<result># Findings: PersonaHub Design Questions vs. multica / clowder-ai

## Q1 — Interceptable permission/approval channel in Claude Code CLI or OpenCode CLI?

**Verdict: Claude Code CLI has a real, documented approval channel (`control_request`/`control_response`) that multica implements but never wires up — genuine dormant code, structurally identical to what you described for Codex. OpenCode's CLI has no analogous message-level protocol; clowder-ai's "auto-approval" file is CLI-flag discovery, not a protocol handler.**

**Claude — dormant control-request handler (real channel, unused):**
`D:\Projects\multica\server\pkg\agent\claude.go:302-338` defines a full handler:
```go
func (b *claudeBackend) handleControlRequest(msg claudeSDKMessage, stdin interface{ Write([]byte) (int, error) }) {
	// Auto-approve all tool uses in autonomous/daemon mode.
	...
	response := map[string]any{
		"type": "control_response",
		"response": map[string]any{
			"subtype": "success", "request_id": msg.RequestID,
			"response": map[string]any{"behavior": "allow", "updatedInput": inputMap},
		},
	}
	...
}
```
`claudeSDKMessage` even has the wire fields for it (`RequestID`, `Request json.RawMessage`, line 360-362). But the stream-reader's dispatch switch in `Execute()` (lines 157-189) only handles `"assistant"`, `"user"`, `"system"`, `"result"`, `"log"` — **there is no `case "control_request"`**. I confirmed via grep that `handleControlRequest` is referenced nowhere except its own definition and a direct unit-test call (`claude_test.go:137`) — it is never invoked from the production message loop. This is the mirror image of `codex.go`'s live handling: in `codex.go`, `handleServerRequest` (line 1314) IS wired into the dispatch (`handleLine` → `handleServerRequest` for any JSON-RPC message with an `id`+`method`) and unconditionally auto-accepts `item/commandExecution/requestApproval` / `applyPatchApproval` / `mcpServer/elicitation/request` (lines 1321-1332). Claude's equivalent exists in code but is inert because `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` (line 488) means the CLI never emits `control_request` in the first place — so multica ships a ready-made interception point that would fire if the bypass flag were removed.

**OpenCode — no message-level protocol found:** `opencodeBackend.processEvents` in `opencode.go` (lines 210-257) only recognizes `text`, `tool_use`, `error`, `step_start`, `step_finish` event types from `opencode run --format json`. There is no request/approval event type in the schema (`opencodeEvent`/`opencodeEventPart`, lines 393-458) and no control-response write-back path (unlike Claude's stdin control_response). OpenCode's non-interactive behavior is governed purely by the `--dangerously-skip-permissions` CLI flag (line 22 blocklist, line 54 injection) plus `OPENCODE_PERMISSION` config — not a runtime message a caller can intercept.

**clowder-ai's `opencode-auto-approval.ts`:** despite the name, this file does **not** implement an approval protocol handler. It's a *capability probe* that runs `opencode run --help` (and hidden `--help` variants) at startup to discover which of `--auto` / `--dangerously-skip-permissions` / `--yolo` the installed OpenCode binary actually supports (`probeOpenCodeAutoApproveSupport`, lines 91-116), then caches the result and injects the correct flag. It also lets a user's own `custom_args`/CLI config override auto-approval (`userControlsOpenCodeAutoApprove`, line 49) rather than forcing it. This is flag discovery/compatibility shimming, not interception of an execution-time approval message.

**Takeaway:** Claude Code's `stream-json` protocol does have a real, interceptable `control_request` channel — multica already has (unused) code for it, so a PersonaHub "ask before risky tool use" feature is a wiring exercise, not new protocol work; OpenCode offers no equivalent channel, so the same feature there can only be built as a CLI-flag/config gate, never a live approval prompt.

---

## Q2 — API key / secret storage: encrypted or plaintext?

**Verdict: Both projects store provider API keys in plaintext, gated only by application-layer access control / masking (multica) or OS file permissions (clowder-ai). Neither uses real encryption-at-rest, a KMS, or an OS keychain.**

**multica:** Credentials travel as a free-form env map on the `agent` row:
`D:\Projects\multica\server\migrations\040_agent_custom_env.up.sql`:
```sql
-- Supports router/proxy (ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL), Bedrock, Vertex AI modes.
ALTER TABLE agent ADD COLUMN custom_env JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}';
```
`internal/handler/agent_env.go` reads/writes this JSONB column directly (`unmarshalCustomEnv`, `UpdateAgentCustomEnv`) — no encrypt/decrypt call anywhere in the path. Protection is entirely procedural: an `envSentinel = "****"` masks values in API responses (never persisted as ciphertext, just redacted on the wire), reveal/update require owner/admin role and write an audit-log row (`GetAgentEnv`/`UpdateAgentEnv`, lines 94-247), and agent-actor tokens are explicitly blocked from these endpoints. But the DB column itself is plaintext JSON — anyone with DB access (or a SQL-injection/backup leak) sees raw keys.

**clowder-ai:** Same pattern, file-based instead of DB-based:
`D:\Projects\clowder-ai\packages\api\src\config\credentials.ts`:
```ts
function writeAll(creds, projectRoot) {
  writeFileAtomic(credPath, `${JSON.stringify(creds, null, 2)}\n`);
  chmodSync(credPath, 0o600);
}
```
`{projectRoot}/.cat-cafe/credentials.json` holds `{ apiKey: "..." }` entries as plain JSON, protected only by `chmod 0o600` (owner-read-only) file permissions — no cipher, no OS keychain API (Keychain/DPAPI/libsecret), no KMS envelope. `catalog-accounts.ts` even documents legacy migrations moving secrets between plaintext files (`provider-profiles.secrets.local.json` → `credentials.json`), always via `JSON.parse`/`writeFileSync`, never through any crypto module. `account-resolver.ts` (`accountToRuntimeProfile`, line 247) reads the key straight off disk via `readCredential`.

**Takeaway:** If PersonaHub needs actual confidentiality guarantees for stored API keys (vs. these projects' "trust the workspace member / trust the local filesystem perms" model), you'll need to add real encryption yourselves — neither reference project has prior art to copy for that.

---

## Q3 — Formal task/workflow message vs. ad-hoc/casual message

**Verdict: clowder-ai explicitly decouples "message" from "invocation" (a message can exist with zero task-state impact); multica routes essentially everything through a task-producing path, but even there, "comment" (always issue-scoped) and "task creation" (conditional) are two separate, decoupled concerns.**

**clowder-ai — messages and invocations are different tables/lifecycles by design.** The code comment in `InvocationRecordStore.ts` says it outright:
`D:\Projects\clowder-ai\packages\api\src\domains\cats\services\stores\ports\InvocationRecordStore.ts:2-3`:
```
* InvocationRecord Store
* 调用状态机：将"消息写入"与"猫调用执行"解耦。   // "decouples message-writing from cat-invocation-execution"
```
`InvocationRecord.targetCats: CatId[]` (line 27) is only populated — and an invocation only ever gets created — when a message actually resolves to one or more target cats (i.e., an `@mention`); its own `state machine` (`invocation-state-machine.ts`) tracks `queued→running→succeeded/failed/canceled`, a lifecycle wholly separate from `MessageStore`'s `StoredMessage`, whose `extra.stream.invocationId` field is optional (`MessageStore.ts:106`). A plain message with no `@mention` (a "casual" note, a whisper, a reply that mentions nobody) is appended to `MessageStore` and simply never produces an `InvocationRecord` — no task state touched at all.

**multica — no free-floating comment, but comments and tasks are decoupled too.** Every `comment` row requires `issue_id NOT NULL` (`001_init.up.sql:99`) — there's no comment outside the formal Issue workflow. But *not every comment creates a task*: `comment.go`'s `triggerTasksForComment` (line 733) only calls `EnqueueTaskForIssue`/`EnqueueTaskForSquadLeader`/`EnqueueTaskForMention` when gating conditions pass (assignee check, not-mentioning-someone-else, not-a-reply-to-a-member-thread) — a comment can be posted purely as conversation with zero task/queue effect. The one channel that is *always* task-producing is Chat: `SendChatMessage` (`chat.go:392-501`) unconditionally calls `h.TaskService.EnqueueChatTask(...)` for every message sent (line 464) — chat has no "just talk, no task" mode, though it also doesn't require an Issue (`issue_id` was made nullable on `agent_task_queue` specifically to support this, `033_chat.up.sql:32-36`).

**Takeaway:** clowder-ai gives you a genuine, low-friction side-channel (message without invocation) baked into its core store design; multica's closest equivalent is "issue comment that doesn't trigger" — real, but every message still lives inside the formal Issue/Task record, and its Chat feature is fully task-tracked with no side-channel at all.

---

## Q4 — Preventing duplicate task/validation creation on a race (automatic vs. manual trigger)

**Verdict: multica resolves this with a DB-level unique partial index as the hard safety net plus an app-level pre-check for the common case; clowder-ai resolves the equivalent race with an in-process per-(thread, cat) mutex around dequeue.**

**multica — re-confirmed migration:**
`D:\Projects\multica\server\migrations\022_task_lifecycle_guards.up.sql`:
```sql
-- Prevent duplicate pending tasks for the same issue (coalescing queue safety net).
-- At most one queued/dispatched task per issue at any time.
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_one_pending_task_per_issue
    ON agent_task_queue (issue_id)
    WHERE status IN ('queued', 'dispatched');
```
This does exactly what you described: it's a Postgres partial unique index, so two INSERTs into `agent_task_queue` for the same issue while one is still `queued`/`dispatched` cannot both succeed — the loser gets a `23505` unique-violation. It was later refined (not replaced in spirit) by `037_fix_pending_task_unique_index.up.sql`, which drops that index and recreates it scoped to `(issue_id, agent_id)` so multiple agents can each have their own pending task on the same issue, but the same agent still can't double-queue.

The explicit "auto trigger vs. manual trigger" collision path is `shouldEnqueueOnComment` in `internal/handler/issue.go:2654-2676`:
```go
// Coalescing queue: allow enqueue when a task is running (so the agent
// picks up new comments on the next cycle) but skip if this agent already
// has a pending task (natural dedup for rapid-fire comments).
hasPending, err := h.Queries.HasPendingTaskForIssueAndAgent(...)
if err != nil || hasPending { return false }
```
This is an application-level pre-check (TOCTOU-prone by itself), with the DB unique index as the atomic backstop if two triggers race past the pre-check simultaneously — the losing `CreateAgentTask` call fails and is swallowed with a warning log (`comment.go:737-739`), i.e. "first committer wins, second silently no-ops," never a duplicate row.

**clowder-ai — in-process per-slot mutex, not a DB constraint (no DB row for the queue at all in the in-memory implementation):**
`D:\Projects\clowder-ai\packages\api\src\domains\cats\services\agents\invocation\QueueProcessor.ts:200-201`:
```ts
/** F108: Per-slot mutex — prevents concurrent double-start per (thread, cat) pair. */
private processingSlots = new Map&lt;string, number&gt;();
```
The two entry points named in the file's own header comment map directly onto your "automatic vs. manual" framing:
```
* - onInvocationComplete（系统级）：invocation 完成后调用，succeeded 时自动出队   // system-level, auto-dequeue
* - processNext（用户级）：co-creator手动触发处理自己的下一条                    // user-level, manual trigger
```
Both paths funnel through the same guarded start sequence (`tryAutoExecute`, lines 714-737):
```ts
if (this.processingSlots.has(sk)) continue;
if (this.deps.invocationTracker.has(threadId, entryCat)) continue;
if (!this.deps.queue.markProcessingById(threadId, entry.id)) continue;  // atomic claim
this.processingSlots.set(sk, Date.now());
```
`markProcessingById` is the atomic claim (single-threaded Node event loop makes the Map-based check-then-set effectively atomic); whichever caller — the automatic completion handler or the manual `processNext` — reaches it first wins the slot, and the other observes it already taken and skips. There's also zombie-slot recovery (`processingSlotTtlMs`, ~2.5× CLI timeout) so a crashed claim doesn't permanently block the pair.

**Takeaway:** multica's answer is "DB unique constraint is the source of truth, app pre-check is just an optimization to avoid noisy failed-insert logs"; clowder-ai's answer is "in-memory mutex per (thread, cat) is the source of truth" — meaning multica's guarantee survives process restarts/multi-instance deployments for free, while clowder-ai's does not (a second API instance would need its own slot map synchronized, e.g. via Redis, to get the same guarantee across processes).</result>

## assistant · 2026-07-12T13:55:51Z

这轮调研质量很高，四个问题基本都有了扎实的证据支撑，可以直接给出结论了：

**Q1（approval 钩子）**：Claude Code 的 `stream-json` 协议里确实有一个真实的 `control_request`/`control_response` 通道——multica 的 `claude.go` 里甚至已经写好了处理函数 `handleControlRequest`，只是没有接入实际的消息分发逻辑（因为默认传了 `--permission-mode bypassPermissions`，Claude Code 根本不会发出这类请求）。这意味着 Claude Code 要做真正的前置拦截是可行的，而且有现成的代码形状可以参考。**OpenCode 则完全没有这类协议通道**，它的免审批完全靠 CLI flag（`--dangerously-skip-permissions`）控制，没有消息级别的请求可以拦截。

**Q2（API key 存储）**：两个项目都是**明文存储**——multica 存在 DB 的 JSONB 列里，只在 API 响应里用"****"打码，不是真加密；clowder-ai 存在本地 JSON 文件里，靠 `chmod 0600` 文件权限保护。两边都没有真正的加密/KMS/OS keychain。

**Q3（workflow-bound vs 咨询性区分）**：clowder-ai 的做法很值得借鉴——它把"消息"和"调用"（Invocation）在数据结构上就解耦了：只有消息里 `@` 到了具体角色、才会创建一条 Invocation 记录进入状态机；没有 `@` 任何人的消息就单纯是对话，不碰任务状态。multica 也类似，评论不是每次都触发任务，有专门的判断函数。

**Q4（手动/自动 validator 撞车）**：multica 用的是 **数据库层唯一约束**（`agent_task_queue` 的 partial unique index，同一 issue 同一时刻只能有一条 pending 记录），应用层的预检查只是为了减少无意义的失败日志，真正兜底的是这条 DB 约束；clowder-ai 用的是**进程内的 per-(thread, cat) 互斥锁**，重启或多实例场景下不生效。

## 我的建议

1. **Q1 关闭**：Claude Code 走真正的前置拦截（复用 multica 的 `control_request` 处理形状，不传 bypass flag，收到危险操作请求时按 escalation 策略回 `deny`）；OpenCode 没有这个通道，继续完全依赖 F002 已定的凭据隔离作为唯一防线，不假装有前置拦截能力。这跟我们之前"凭据隔离是主防线、approval hook 是锦上添花"的判断完全吻合，只是现在知道了这个"锦上添花"在三个 adapter 上的能力不对等。

2. **Q2 给出方向性建议，不逐字照抄**：两个参考项目都走了"信任本地文件权限"这条捷径，但 PersonaHub 是桌面软件，更好的选择是**优先用 OS 级 keychain**（Windows Credential Manager / macOS Keychain，通常有现成的轻量库可以跨平台调用），实现麻烦的话再退化到"本地文件 + 权限限制"这种两个项目都用的兜底方案——不建议直接照抄明文存储。具体用哪个库留给 design.md。

3. **Q3 用"@ 是否命中当前期望角色"作为主要判定信号，而不是额外加一个 UI 开关**：如果用户 @ 的 agent 正好匹配 Issue 当前阶段期望的角色（比如 Validating 阶段 @ 一个配置了 validator 能力的 adapter），就是 workflow-bound；否则就是咨询性。这样比"每次都要手动声明这是不是正式流程"摩擦更小，也是 clowder-ai 已经验证过的模式。

4. **Q4 关闭，采用 DB 唯一约束方案**：给 Run 表加一条 partial unique index（同一 Issue 的 validator 角色，同一时刻只能有一条 `queued`/`running` 记录），跟 F001 给 primary Thread 加唯一约束是同一个思路，比进程内互斥锁更稳（重启、以后 daemon 化都不受影响）。谁先创建谁赢，另一个直接失败返回明确错误。

## Findings

### Q1 — Command/file-change evidence: structured events vs. reconstructed diff

**Verdict: split behavior in both projects — command history comes from structured CLI events; file-changed evidence is reconstructed on demand via git, not derived from those events.**

**multica** (`D:\Projects\multica\server\pkg\agent\codex.go:1542-1606`, `handleItemNotification`): Codex's `item/started`/`item/completed` notifications for `commandExecution` and `fileChange` are turned directly into `Message{Type: MessageToolUse/MessageToolResult, Tool: "exec_command"/"patch_apply", ...}` — pure structured-event pass-through, no git involved. These messages flow into the daemon's drain loop (`D:\Projects\multica\server\internal\daemon\daemon.go:3161-3179`, the `MessageToolUse`/`MessageToolResult` cases) and get batched into `TaskMessageData` rows shipped via `ReportTaskMessages` — this is multica's entire "activity timeline"/tool-history mechanism. I found **no** `git diff`/`git status`/`--name-status` invocation anywhere in `server/internal`, `server/cmd`, or `server/pkg` (verified by grep for literal `"git diff"`/`"git status"` across the whole tree — zero hits outside comments). `execenv/git.go` only does worktree lifecycle (`rev-parse`, `fetch`, `worktree add/remove`), never diffing. So multica has **no changed-files reconstruction step at all** — command evidence is 100% structured-event-driven, and there's no equivalent file-change summary feature.

**clowder-ai**: command/tool-call history is captured from structured `tool_use`/`tool_result` `AgentMessage`s and converted via `toStoredToolEvent()` (`D:\Projects\clowder-ai\packages\api\src\domains\cats\services\agents\routing\route-helpers.ts:340-383`) into a `StoredToolEvent` used for the "Hub history view" — again, structured-event-driven, no git involved. But the **file-changed evidence** for the workspace diff viewer is entirely reconstructed after the fact: `D:\Projects\clowder-ai\packages\api\src\routes\workspace.ts:516-619` (`GET /api/workspace/diff`) runs `git status --porcelain -uall` to list changed files, then `git diff HEAD --unified=3 -- &lt;pathspec&gt;` (falling back to `git diff --cached`, then `git diff --no-index /dev/null &lt;file&gt;` for untracked files) computed live on each request — nothing is persisted from tool events for this purpose. `RealGitRunner.ts`/`GitRefSnapshotCollector.ts` (feat-trajectory) are a separate, unrelated system: they use `git ls-remote`/`git show`/`git log` for a cron-driven feature-branch lifecycle tracker, not command/file-change evidence for a given agent run.

**Takeaway:** command/tool history is structured-event-driven in both; file-changed evidence, where it exists at all (clowder-ai only), is always a live git reconstruction, never derived from the event stream — treat "what changed" as a diff-computed view, not part of the durable event log.

### Q2 — Distinct Artifact storage abstraction?

**Verdict: multica has a real managed-storage Attachment entity; clowder-ai has only a lightweight, path/URL-based derived reference concept.**

**multica**: `Attachment` is a first-class DB entity (`D:\Projects\multica\server\pkg\db\generated\models.go:102-116`) with its own table (`029_attachment.up.sql`), fields `workspace_id`, `issue_id`, `comment_id`, `chat_session_id`, `chat_message_id`, `uploader_type` (`CHECK (uploader_type IN ('member', 'agent'))` — i.e. explicitly supports agent-generated files, not just human uploads), `filename`, `url`, `content_type`, `size_bytes`. Content is pushed through a `Storage` interface (`D:\Projects\multica\server\internal\storage\storage.go`) backed by S3 or local disk (`storage/s3.go`, `storage/local.go`), with a dedicated CLI surface `cmd_attachment.go` and a preview-proxy endpoint (`GET /api/attachments/{id}/content`). This is a heavier, genuinely managed storage system, decoupled from the message stream (comments/chat messages just reference attachment rows by FK).

**clowder-ai**: "artifact" is a lightweight, computed reference type, not a storage system. `RecentArtifact`/`ThreadArtifactDTO` (`D:\Projects\clowder-ai\packages\api\src\domains\cats\services\agents\routing\artifact-tracking.ts`, `thread-artifacts-aggregator.ts`) is `{ type: 'pr'|'file'|'plan'|'image'|...; ref: string; label: string; updatedAt; updatedBy }` — `ref` is just a file path or PR number, aggregated on the fly from (1) rich message blocks (`file`/`media_gallery`/`diff` blocks that themselves carry a `url` pointing at wherever the media was uploaded), (2) PR-tracking tasks, and (3) a capped 20-entry in-memory "ledger" persisted as part of thread memory — deduplicated by `ref`, no dedicated table/blob store of its own. Underlying media storage lives in `ConnectorMediaService.ts`, separate from this abstraction.

**Takeaway:** multica models artifacts as owned, storage-backed records with agent/human provenance; clowder-ai only tracks lightweight pointers into other stores (messages, PR tasks) — pick multica's model if PersonaHub needs durable, queryable "what did the agent produce" records.

### Q3 — Markdown/report export mechanism

**Verdict: clowder-ai generates Markdown in-memory and streams it as an HTTP response; multica has no export/report feature at all.**

**clowder-ai** (`D:\Projects\clowder-ai\packages\api\src\routes\export.ts`): `GET /api/export/thread/:threadId?format=md|txt` calls `formatThreadAsMarkdown(thread, messages)` — a pure string-builder function — and returns the string directly as the Fastify response body with `Content-Type: text/markdown` and `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="thread-&lt;id&gt;.md"`. No file is ever written to the backend filesystem; the "download" is purely an HTTP response with a disposition header telling the browser to save it.

**multica**: I found no `Export`/`Report` route or CLI command anywhere (`grep -rn "func.*[Ee]xport|/export"` across `server/internal` and `server/cmd` returned nothing). The closest thing, `buildSquadLeaderBriefing`/`buildSquadRoster` (`D:\Projects\multica\server\internal\handler\squad_briefing.go`), builds a Markdown-ish string via `strings.Builder`, but it's used to inject a "briefing" section into an agent's runtime context/system prompt (`handler/daemon.go:1183,1501`), not delivered to a user as a report/export. Also no `os.WriteFile`/file-write path was found for report content.

**Takeaway:** if PersonaHub needs "export as Markdown," clowder-ai's pattern (build string in memory, return as HTTP response with Content-Disposition) is the model to copy; multica offers no precedent here.

### Q4 — Structured reviewer/validator output contract?

**Verdict: mixed within clowder-ai — a strict zod-validated JSON contract exists for one kind of "verdict" (harness-eval), but the actual PR-review/merge-gate flow parses freeform Markdown via regex, not JSON. multica has no reviewer-output contract of any kind.**

**clowder-ai PR/merge-gate review** is freeform: `cat-cafe-skills/merge-gate/SKILL.md` is a procedural SOP for the *acting* agent (it must find review "放行/LGTM/通过" signals and confirm 0 unresolved P1/P2 before merging) — not a machine-parsed schema. The actual code that extracts structure from review text is `D:\Projects\clowder-ai\packages\api\src\infrastructure\email\severity-parser.ts`: `parseSeverity(body)` runs three regexes over a PR comment body (shields.io badge `P[0-2]`, line-leading `[P0]`/`[P1]`/`[P2]`, or `P0:`/`P1:`/`P2:`), after stripping fenced code + blockquotes to avoid false positives, and returns `'P0'|'P1'|'P2'|null`. `getMaxSeverity()` scans all comments/decisions and returns the worst found. `ReviewFeedbackRouter.ts` uses this: if `null` (no marker found), no severity header is added — the raw comment is just relayed as `[UNTRUSTED EXTERNAL CONTENT]` text for the agent to interpret itself; there's no hard failure/rejection path, just "no severity detected."

A **separate**, unrelated system — the harness-eval "verdict" pipeline — does have a strict schema: `verdictHandoffPacketSchema` (zod, `D:\Projects\clowder-ai\packages\api\src\infrastructure\harness-eval\verdict-handoff.ts`) requires fields like `verdict: z.enum(['delete_sunset','build','fix','keep_observe'])`, `rootCauseHypothesis.confidence: z.enum(['low','medium','high'])`, evidence refs, etc., with a `superRefine` cross-field rule. `parseVerdictHandoffPacket()` throws on failure, and the caller (`publish-verdict.ts:85-96`) catches it and returns `{ status: 400, error: 'invalid_packet', detail: message }` — clean reject-with-diagnostic. But this is a feature-health/deprecation "verdict," not a code-review finding.

**multica**: no reviewer/validator agent output contract exists at all — `grep`s for `severity`/`Severity` in multica turn up only unrelated notification/inbox severity fields, and "review" only shows up as a human-set `in_review` issue status string, never as a parsed agent output.

**Takeaway:** don't assume "structured reviewer output" is a solved pattern — even clowder-ai's real code-review gate is regex-over-freeform-Markdown with silent no-match handling; only its unrelated eval/verdict pipeline has a true strict-JSON contract with reject-on-parse-failure semantics.

### Q5 — Is `055_task_lease_and_retry` an automatic-after-failure retry, and under what bounds?

**Verdict: fully automatic, but strictly infrastructure-scoped — it retries crash/orphan/timeout-class failures, never a review rejection or "agent produced bad output," and it's bounded by `max_attempts` with no human action required (or possible) to trigger it.**

The gate is `retryableReasons` (`D:\Projects\multica\server\internal\service\task.go:1320-1329`):
```go
var retryableReasons = map[string]bool{
    "runtime_offline":           true,
    "runtime_recovery":          true,
    "timeout":                   true,
    "codex_semantic_inactivity": true,
}
```
with the comment: *"Agent-side errors (compile failures, model rejections, etc.) are intentionally excluded — those are real problems that the user should see, not infrastructure flakiness."* `agent_error` (the default classifier) is deliberately **not** in this set, so a task the agent completed-but-failed (or that a reviewer rejected, if such a concept existed) is never auto-retried.

`MaybeRetryFailedTask` (`task.go:1352-1401`) enforces, in order: `status == "failed"`, reason ∈ `retryableReasons`, `attempt &lt; max_attempts` (logs `"task auto-retry skipped: budget exhausted"` and returns nil otherwise), not an autopilot-owned task (`AutopilotRunID` — autopilot has its own separate re-run cadence), and must have an `issue_id` or `chat_session_id`. If all pass, it calls `CreateRetryTask(parent.ID)`, which creates a new `agent_task_queue` row with `parent_task_id` set, `attempt = parent.attempt + 1`, inheriting session/work_dir for resume-safe reasons.

Triggers: this fires automatically from two paths — (1) `FailTask` calls it inline the moment any task transitions to `failed` (`task.go:1267`), and (2) `HandleFailedTasks` (`task.go:1534-1553`), invoked by the runtime heartbeat sweeper (`cmd/server/runtime_sweeper.go:149-155,239-279`, stale-heartbeat/crashed-daemon detection) and by `RecoverOrphanedTasks` (`handler/task_lifecycle.go:14-56`, daemon-restart orphan recovery). Both are crash/liveness-detection code paths, not review/QA outcomes.

By contrast, **manual rerun** (`RerunIssue`, `handler/task_lifecycle.go:98-153`) is the human-action path — it sets `force_fresh_session=true` specifically because *"a user clicking rerun has just judged the prior output bad — replaying the same conversation would replay the same poisoned state,"* explicitly distinguished from automatic retry which "intentionally inherits the session — that path handles infrastructure failures, not bad output." When `max_attempts` is exceeded, the auto-retry path simply stops (logs and returns nil) — no escalation is coded; the task just stays `failed` until a human manually reruns it.

**Takeaway:** multica's lease/retry columns are purely a crash/timeout auto-recovery mechanism — "reviewer said rework this" is not modeled anywhere in multica and would need new machinery (a distinct failure_reason/status and its own retry-eligibility path) if PersonaHub wants automatic retry-after-review-rejection.</result>

研究结果很有价值，几条发现和 F003/F004 现有假设吻合，但有两条发现了值得注意的细节差异。逐条给建议：

## F003（Development Trace）

**Q1（关闭）**：multica 和 clowder-ai 的"命令执行历史"**都是**从 Codex/Claude/OpenCode 各自的结构化事件（`item/commandExecution`、`tool_use`/`tool_result`）直接转换来的，没有一个项目靠解析 stdout 文本兜底。这比 F003 原来"推荐优先 adapter structured event"的表述更有把握了，两个参考项目独立验证了同一个选择，可以直接确认为结论。

**Q2（关闭，但有个重要细节要调整设计）**：multica **完全没有** file-change 功能；clowder-ai 有，但它是**实时按需算的**——用户打开 diff 查看器时才跑一次 `git status`/`git diff`，不从任何事件流里推导，也不落盘持久化。这和 F003 现在的设计不太一样：F003 的 `FR-003` 是"Run 结束时把文件变更摘要**写成一条 ThreadEvent 持久化下来**"。我建议**不要照抄 clowder 的"实时算"模式**——它适合一个交互式 diff 查看器，但 PersonaHub 的 evidence 要求的是"以后还能查这次 Run 到底改了什么"，如果只是实时算，等后面的 Run 又改了同样的文件，就再也无法准确复现"这一轮具体改了什么"。F003 现有的"Run 完成时生成并持久化快照"这个设计，其实比 clowder 的做法更契合 PersonaHub 自己的证据留存要求，应该保留，不是被两个项目"证伪"了。git diff/status 作为主要机制这一点两个项目也没反对，非 git fallback scan 继续保留。

**Q3（关闭）**：multica 的 Attachment 是一个很重的托管存储系统（独立表、S3/本地存储后端、上传者类型区分）；clowder-ai 的"artifact"只是一个轻量指针（文件路径或 PR 号），聚合展示、不单独落盘。这正好对应 F003 原来的推荐——现在用 clowder 这种轻量引用模式（`evidence_refs` + inline/db record），multica 那种重型托管存储留给 v0.3 真正需要的时候再考虑。

**Q4（关闭）**：clowder-ai 的 export 就是内存里拼一个 Markdown 字符串，直接当 HTTP 响应体返回（带 `Content-Disposition` 让浏览器触发下载），后端不落盘；multica 压根没有 export 功能。确认 F003 原有推荐（后端生成内容、UI 触发下载）。

## F004（Autonomous Validation）

**Q1（维持原判断，不采纳 clowder 的更宽松做法）**：clowder-ai 真实的 PR review/merge-gate 流程其实**不是**严格 JSON——是对自由格式 Markdown 用正则找 P0/P1/P2 severity 标记，找不到就静默当"没问题"处理，不会硬性拒绝。但这个宽松处理适合 clowder 的场景，因为**始终有人或 agent 在读原始文本**去理解模糊内容；F004 要的是**完全自动**决定 Done/Blocked，没有人在中间兜底解读，所以"解析失败必须 Blocked"这个更严格的立场是对的，不应该学 clowder 变宽松——这是两个项目需求本质不同，不是我们的设计有问题。

**Q2（关闭，高置信度）**：这条被 multica 自己的生产系统直接验证了。它的 `max_attempts`/`attempt` 重试机制**明确只覆盖基础设施类失败**（进程崩溃、超时、runtime 离线），代码注释原文写着"agent 自身的错误(编译失败、模型拒绝等)是有意排除在外的——那些是真实问题,应该让用户看到,而不是基础设施抖动"。人工触发的 rerun 还专门重置了 session（`force_fresh_session=true`），原因是"用户点 rerun 就是判断了上一次输出不行，复用同一个对话只会复现同样的坏状态"。这跟 F004 现在"validation fail 不自动开下一轮，等用户触发"的决定是完全一致的独立验证。

**Q3**：已经被 F005 实际回答了——一旦 Claude Code/OpenCode 接入，validator 可以用真正不同的 provider，比"同 provider 不同 model"更可信；"同 provider 不同 model"作为没配置多 adapter 时的兜底方案保留。

**Q4**：两个项目都没有类似"Evidence Summary"这种独立于原始导出之外的结构化完成记录，这条留给 PersonaHub 自己判断，维持现有推荐即可，没有新证据改变它。

要不要我现在把这些结论写回 F003、F004 的 spec.md？
